\mathbf{l}\in\mathcal{A}
.
If the boundary cylinder
\pi(D)
does not have
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}
as a boundary component, the same argument as we used in Case 2 proves

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.

Suppose that the boundary cylinder
\pi(D)
has
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}
as a boundary component. Recall that
\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
is the sum of the lozenge vector field
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
with some eruption cocycles. We have already established in Case 1 that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime\prime}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.

for any eruption cocycle
\nu^{\prime\prime}
, so

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\big),

where recall that
\zeta^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
is the
(\rho,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{J})
-tangent cocycle associated to
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}([\rho])
. Since
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2})=0=\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{3})
by Proposition
7.18
(1), (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) imply that regardless of whether
D
is consistent or not, we have

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{1}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}^{2}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=\tr\big(\widetilde{\sigma}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(u_{1})_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2}-v_{3})_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\big),

where recall that
\sigma^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
is the
(\rho,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{J})
-tangent cocycle associated to
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}([\rho])
.

Let
P(1)
and
P(2)
be the pairs of pants of
\mathcal{P}
that lie to the right and left of
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}
respectively. Then for both
m=1,2
, let
{\bf P}(m):=(P(m),\widehat{c})
, and let
(\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(m),\widehat{\mathbf{b}}^{\prime}(m))
be the pair of non-edge barriers associated to
{\bf P}(m)
. Let
{\bf c}
,
{\bf b}(m)
and
{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
be lifts to
\widetilde{S}
of
\widehat{\bf c}
,
\widehat{\bf b}(m)
and
\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
respectively, so that
{\bf b}(m)
intersects
\delta_{P(m)}
,
{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
intersects
\delta^{\prime}_{P(m)}
, and the three barriers
{\bf c}
,
{\bf b}(m)
, and
{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
share a common endpoint. Then let
{\bf x}(m)
and
{\bf x}^{\prime}(m)
be the triples associated to
\widehat{\bf b}(m)
and
\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
respectively, and let
{\bf r}
be the pair associated to
{\bf c}
.

Suppose that
\pi(D)
lies in
P(1)
. Then by Proposition
5.18
,

\widetilde{\sigma}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(u_{1})_{{\mathbf{r}},{\rm diag}}=-\frac{1}{2}D^{\mathbf{k}}_{\xi_{\rho}({\mathbf{r}})}.

Also, by Proposition
5.17
and (
6.6
)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2}-v_{3})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(-A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}+A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}(1,-1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}-A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}+A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}(1,0,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}\right)

\displaystyle-A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}(0,-1,1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}+A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}(-1,0,1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(-A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}+A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}(1,-1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}-A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}+A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}(1,0,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}\right)

\displaystyle-A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}(0,1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}+A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}(-1,1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}+A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}(1,-1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}-A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}+A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}(1,0,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}

\displaystyle-A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}(0,-1,1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}+A^{\mathbf{l}_{3}(-1,0,1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}-A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}(0,1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))}+A^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}_{2}(-1,1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}(1))},

where we recall that

•

for any
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{A}
, we denote
\mathbf{k}_{1}:=(0,k_{1},k_{2})
,
\mathbf{k}_{2}:=(k_{2},0,k_{1})
,
\mathbf{k}_{3}:=(k_{1},k_{2},0)
,
\bar{\mathbf{k}}_{1}:=(0,k_{2},k_{1})
,
\bar{\mathbf{k}}_{2}:=(k_{1},0,k_{2})
, and
\bar{\mathbf{k}}_{3}:=(k_{2},k_{1},0)
, and

•

for any
{\bf i}=(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})\in\mathcal{B}
and any triple of integers
(a_{1},a_{3},a_{3})
, we denote
\mathbf{i}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}):=(i_{1}+a_{1},i_{2}+a_{2},i_{3}+a_{3})
.

So, Lemma
5.14
implies that if for any
{\bf k}=(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{A}
and any pair of integers
(a_{1},a_{2})
, we denote
{\bf k}(a_{1},a_{2}):=(k_{1}+a_{1},k_{2}+a_{2})
, then

(7.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2}-v_{3})_{{\mathbf{r}},{\rm diag}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4D^{\mathbf{l}(-1,1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}-8D^{\mathbf{l}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r}(1))}+4D^{\mathbf{l}(1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2M^{\mathbf{l}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}+2M^{\mathbf{l}(1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}.

Similarly, if
\pi(D)
lies in
P(2)
, then

\widetilde{\sigma}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(u_{1})_{{\mathbf{r}},{\rm diag}}=\widetilde{\sigma}^{\bar{\mathbf{k}}}_{\widehat{\bar{\mathbf{c}}}}(u_{1})_{{\mathbf{r}},{\rm diag}}=-\frac{1}{2}D^{\bar{\mathbf{k}}}_{\xi_{\rho}({\mathbf{r}})}

and

\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{l}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2}-v_{3})_{{\mathbf{r}},{\rm diag}}=\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}}_{\widehat{\bar{\mathbf{c}}}}(v_{2}-v_{3})_{{\mathbf{r}},{\rm diag}}=-2M^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}+2M^{\bar{\mathbf{l}}(1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}.

Thus, regardless of whether
\pi(D)
lies in
P(1)
or
P(2)
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{2}&\text{if }\mathbf{l}=\mathbf{k};\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

This shows that if
\mathbf{l}\neq\mathbf{k}
, then the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is zero. On the other hand, when
\mathbf{l}=\mathbf{k}
, then of all the fundamental blocks
D
that is the union of external triangles in
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}
, only two of them have the property that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=\frac{1}{2},

while the rest of them have the property that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0,

and so the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is
1
.
∎

Proposition 7.21
.

Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{i}}_{\mathbf{P}}
for some marked pair of pants
\mathbf{P}
and some
\mathbf{i}:=(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})\in\mathcal{B}
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be any eruption, hexagon or length vector field. Then

\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\text{if }\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{i}}_{\mathbf{P}};\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

The proof is divided into the following cases.

Case 1:
\mathcal{X}
is an eruption vector field.
Observe that
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}
are both skew-symmetric, so Lemma
7.13
(1) implies that the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is zero. Let
D
be a fundamental block, and let
u_{1},\dots,u_{5},v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
be as defined in Notation
7.15
. By Proposition
7.18
(2),
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2}-v_{3})
and
\widetilde{\nu}(v_{2}-v_{3})
are both nilpotent, so Lemma
7.16
(1) and Lemma
7.17
(1) imply that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{1}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}^{2}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.

Since
D
is arbitrary, the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is also zero.

Case 2:
\mathcal{X}
is a length vector field.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
, where
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{P}^{o}
and
\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{A}
. By definition,
\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
is the sum of the lozenge vector field
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
with some eruption cocycles, so Case 1 implies that

\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)=\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}([\rho])\right).

We thus need to show that
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}([\rho])\right)=0
.

Let
P(1)
and
P(2)
be the pairs of pants of
\mathcal{P}
that share
\widehat{\bf c}
as a common boundary component, such that
P(1)
and
P(2)
lie to the right and left of
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}
respectively.

Case 2.1:
\widehat{\bf c}
is not a boundary component of
P
.
For any triangle
\delta\in\mathbb{T}_{\rm lift}
, note that either
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})=0=\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,2})
, or
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,1})=0=\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})
. Thus,
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}([\rho])\right)=0
.

Case 2.2:
\widehat{\bf c}
is a boundary component of
P
.
By the symmetries of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{i}}_{\mathbf{P}}
and
\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
, we may assume that
{\bf P}=(P(1),\widehat{c})
.

If
\delta\in\mathbb{T}_{\rm lift}
is a triangle such that
\pi(\delta)
does not lie in
P(1)
, then by definition,
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})=0=\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,2})
, so
\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0
. Also, if
D
is a fundamental block and
u_{1},\dots,u_{5},v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
are the edges of
\mathbb{T}(D)
as defined in Notation
7.15
, then Proposition
7.18
(2) states that
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2}-v_{3})
is a nilpotent endomorphism and
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(u_{1})=0
. Hence, Lemma
7.16
(2) implies that if
D
is consistent, then

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.

However, if
D
is inconsistent, (
7.2
) implies that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{3})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{3})\big)-\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2})\big).

Thus, if
v_{2}
and
v_{3}
are the internal edges in the fundamental block
D
such that
\pi(D)
is the inconsistent boundary cylinder in
P=P(1)
, then

\displaystyle\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}([\rho])\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)-\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2})\big)

\displaystyle-\left(\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P}^{\prime},1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta_{P}^{\prime},2})\big)-\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{3})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{3})\big)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)-2\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2})\big),

where the second equality is a consequence of Lemma
7.13
(2) and the observation that
\nu^{\prime}
is skew-symmetric in
P
while
\zeta^{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathbf{c}}
is symmetric in
P
. To finish the proof in this case, it is now sufficient to show that

\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)-\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2})\big)=0.

Let
(\widehat{\mathbf{b}},\widehat{\mathbf{b}}^{\prime})
be the pair of non-edge barriers associated to
{\bf P}
, let
{\bf b}
and
{\bf b}^{\prime}
be lifts to
\widetilde{S}
of
\widehat{\bf b}
and
\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime}
respectively, so that
{\bf b}
intersects
\delta_{P}
,
{\bf b}^{\prime}
intersects
\delta^{\prime}_{P}
, and let
{\bf x}
and
{\bf x}^{\prime}
be the triples associated to
\widehat{\bf b}
and
\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime}
respectively. By cyclically permuting the enumeration of the three vertices of
\mathbb{T}
in the interior of
P
if necessary, we may ensure that
e_{\delta_{P},1}
intersects
{\bf b}
. Then by (
6.6
)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}A^{\mathbf{i}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x})},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}A^{\mathbf{i}_{+}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{+})},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathbf{c}}(e_{\delta_{P},2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{-})}^{\mathbf{k}_{1}(1,0,-1)}+A_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{-})}^{\mathbf{k}_{1}(1,-1,0)},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A^{\mathbf{k}_{2}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{+})}+A^{\mathbf{k}_{2}(-1,0,1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{+})}-A^{\mathbf{k}_{2}(-1,1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{+})}+A^{\mathbf{k}_{2}(0,1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{+})}.

Applying Lemma
6.7
gives

\displaystyle\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)=\frac{1}{2}\bigg([\min\{i_{1}-k_{2}+1,1-i_{2}\}]_{+}-[\min\{i_{1}-k_{2},1-i_{2}\}]_{+}\bigg)=0

and

\displaystyle\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\min\{i_{2},k_{2}\}-\min\{i_{2},k_{2}-1\}+\min\{i_{2},k_{2}-1\}-\min\{i_{2},k_{2}\}\bigg)

\displaystyle=0.

Case 3:
\mathcal{X}
is a hexagon vector field.
Let
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{H}^{\bf j}_{\bf Q}
, where
{\bf Q}
be a marked pair of pants given by
\mathcal{P}
and
{\bf j}\in\mathcal{B}
. By Proposition
7.18
(2),(3), both
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(k_{2}-k_{3})
and
\widetilde{\nu}(k_{2}-k_{3})
are nilpotent, so Lemma
7.16
(1) and Lemma
7.17
(1) imply that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\left(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,2})\right)=0.

for any fundamental block
D
. In particular, the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is zero. Thus, it is sufficient to show that the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is
1
if
\mathbf{Q}=\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{j}=\mathbf{i}
, and is zero otherwise. Let
P
and
Q
be the pairs of pants underlying
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
respectively.

Case 3.1:
P\neq Q
.
Observe that for any pair of pants
R
of
\mathcal{P}
, either
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{R}^{\prime},1})=0=\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{R}^{\prime},2})
or
\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{R}^{\prime},1})=0=\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{R}^{\prime},2})
, so the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is zero when
P\neq Q
.

Case 3.2:
P=Q
.

By the symmetries of
\mathcal{E}^{\bf i}_{\bf P}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\bf j}_{\bf Q}
, we may assume that
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{Q}
, and that the pair
(\widehat{\bf b},\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime})
of non-edge barriers associated to
{\bf P}
satisfy the property that there is a lift
{\bf b}
of
\widehat{\bf b}
that intersects
e_{\delta_{P},1}
and a lift
{\bf b}^{\prime}
of
\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime}
that intersects
e_{\delta_{P}^{\prime},1}
. Let
{\bf x}
and
{\bf x}^{\prime}
be the triples associated to
{\bf b}
and
{\bf b}^{\prime}
respectively.

By (
6.6
),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}A^{\mathbf{i}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x})}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{P},2})=

\displaystyle-A^{\mathbf{j}_{-}(-1,0,1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{-})}+A^{\mathbf{j}_{-}(0,-1,1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{-})}-A^{\mathbf{j}_{-}(1,-1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{-})}

\displaystyle+A^{\mathbf{j}_{-}(1,0,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{-})}-A^{\mathbf{j}_{-}(0,1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{-})}+A^{\mathbf{j}_{-}(-1,1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}_{-})}.

Thus, by Lemma
6.7
(1)

\displaystyle\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg([\min\{j_{3}-1-i_{3},i_{1}-j_{1}\}]_{+}-[\min\{j_{3}-i_{3},i_{1}-j_{1}+1\}]_{+}

\displaystyle+[\min\{j_{3}+1-i_{3},i_{1}-j_{1}+1\}]_{+}-[\min\{j_{3}+1-i_{3},i_{1}-j_{1}\}]_{+}

\displaystyle+[\min\{j_{3}-i_{3},i_{1}-j_{1}-1\}]_{+}-[\min\{j_{3}-1-i_{3},i_{1}-j_{1}-1\}]_{+}\bigg)

From this, it is straightforward to observe that if
j_{3}-i_{3}\geq i_{1}-j_{1}+1
and
j_{3}-i_{3}\leq i_{1}-j_{1}-1
, then
\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)=0
.
On the other hand, if
j_{3}-i_{3}=i_{1}-j_{1}
, then the above formula specializes to

\displaystyle\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg([i_{1}-j_{1}-1]_{+}-[i_{1}-j_{1}]_{+}+[i_{1}-j_{1}+1]_{+}-[i_{1}-j_{1}]_{+}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}&\text{if }i_{1}=j_{1};\cr 0&\text{if }i_{1}\neq j_{1}\end{cases}

We have thus proven that

\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}&\text{if }\mathbf{i}=\mathbf{j};\cr 0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Since
\mathcal{E}^{\bf i}_{\bf P}
is skew-symmetric in
P
and
\mathcal{H}^{\bf j}_{\bf P}
is symmetric in
P
, by Lemma
7.13
(2),

\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P}^{\prime},1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{P}^{\prime},2})\big)=\begin{cases}-\frac{1}{2}&\text{if }\mathbf{i}=\mathbf{j};\cr 0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

since the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is

\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P},1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{P},2})\big)-\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta_{P}^{\prime},1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta_{P}^{\prime},2})\big),

it follows that the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is
1
if
\mathbf{i}=\mathbf{j}
, and is zero otherwise.
∎

Proposition 7.22
.

If
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is a hexagon vector field and let
\mathcal{X}
is a hexagon or length vector field, then

\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)=0.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{i}}_{\mathbf{P}}
for some marked pair of pants
\mathbf{P}
and some
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})\in\mathcal{B}
. We prove the proposition for the cases when
\nu
is a hexagon vector field and a length vector field separately.

Case 1:
\mathcal{X}
is a hexagon vector field.
Observe that
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}
are both symmetric in every pair of pants of
\mathcal{P}
, so Lemma
7.13
(1) tells us that the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is zero. On the other hand, for any fundamental block
D
, if we let
u_{1},\dots,u_{5},v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
be as defined in Notation
7.15
, then Proposition
7.18
(3) states that both
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{3}-v_{2})
and
\widetilde{\nu}(v_{3}-v_{2})
are nilpotent. Lemma
7.16
(1) and Lemma
7.17
(1) then imply that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0,

so the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)
is also zero.

Case 2:
\nu
is a length vector field.
Let
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
for some oriented, non-isolated edge
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{P}^{o}
and some
\mathbf{k}:=(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{A}
. Let
P
be the pair of pants underlying
\mathbf{P}
, and let
\widehat{c}
be the non-isolated edge in
\mathcal{P}
underlying
\widehat{\bf c}
.

Case 2.1:
\widehat{c}
is not a boundary component of
P
.
In this case, observe that for any triangle
\delta\in\mathbb{T}_{\rm lift}
, either
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})=0=\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,2})
or
\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,1})=0=\widetilde{\nu}(e_{\delta,2})
. Thus,
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)=0
.

Case 2.2:
\widehat{c}
is a boundary component of
P
.
Let
P(1)
and
P(2)
be the two pairs of pants of
\mathcal{P}
that share
\bf c
as a common boundary component, and lie to the right and left of
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}
respectively. For both
m=1,2
, let
{\bf P}(m):=(P(m),\widehat{c})
. By the symmetries of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{i}}_{\mathbf{P}}
and
\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
, we may assume without loss of generality that
{\bf P}={\bf P}(1)
. Let
(\widehat{\mathbf{b}},\widehat{\mathbf{b}}^{\prime})
be the pair of non-edge barriers associated to
{\bf P}
, let
{\bf b}
and
{\bf b}^{\prime}
be lifts to
\widetilde{S}
of
\widehat{\bf b}
and
\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime}
respectively, so that
{\bf b}
intersects
\delta_{P}
,
{\bf b}^{\prime}
intersects
\delta^{\prime}_{P}
. Let
{\bf x}
and
{\bf x}^{\prime}
be the triples associated to
\widehat{\bf b}
and
\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime}
respectively. By cyclically permuting the enumeration of the three vertices of
\mathbb{T}
in the interior of
P
if necessary, we may ensure that
e_{\delta_{P},1}
intersects
{\bf b}
and
e_{\delta^{\prime}_{P},1}
intersects
{\bf b}^{\prime}
.

By definition,

\mathcal{Y}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}^{\mathbf{k}}-\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}=2\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{k}_{3}(0,-1,1)}_{\mathbf{P}(1)}-2\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{k}_{3}(-1,0,1)}_{\mathbf{P}(1)}+2\mathcal{E}^{\bar{\mathbf{k}}_{3}(0,-1,1)}_{\mathbf{P}(2)}-2\mathcal{E}^{\bar{\mathbf{k}}_{3}(-1,0,1)}_{\mathbf{P}(2)},

Hence, by Proposition
7.21
, we know that

\omega_{[\rho]}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}-\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}(-1,0,1)=\mathbf{i};\\
-2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}(0,-1,1)=\mathbf{i};\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

To finish the proof, it is thus sufficient to show that

\omega_{[\rho]}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}=\mathbf{i}(1,0,-1);\\
2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}=\mathbf{i}(0,1,-1);\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Observe from their definitions that both
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}
are symmetric in every pair of pants given by
\mathcal{P}
. Thus, Lemma
7.13
implies that the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}})
is zero. To compute the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}})
, let
D
be a fundamental block, and let
u_{1},\dots,u_{5},v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
be as defined in Notation
7.15
. If
\pi(D)
does not lie in
P
, then observe that
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})=0
for all
\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)
, which implies

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\left(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\right)=0.

If
\pi(D)
lies in
P
but does not have
\widehat{c}
as a boundary component, then Proposition
7.18
(3),(4) tell us that
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2}-v_{3})
and
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2}-v_{3})
are both nilpotent. Thus Lemma
7.16
(1) and Lemma
7.17
(1) imply

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.

Now, suppose that
\pi(D)
lies in
P
and has
\widehat{c}
as a boundary component. Let
{\bf c}
be the lift in
\widetilde{S}
of
\widehat{\bf c}
that intersects
\partial D
, and let
{\bf r}=(r_{1},r_{2})
be the pair associated to
\mathbf{c}
. Then
D
is necessarily a consistent fundamental block. By Proposition
5.18
and Lemma
5.14
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(u_{1})_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\text{if }i_{3}>1;\\
-\left(A^{\mathbf{i}(0,1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x})}\right)_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}+\left(A^{\mathbf{i}(1,0,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x})}\right)_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}&\text{if }i_{3}=1.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\text{if }i_{3}>1;\\
-2D^{(i_{1},i_{2}+1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}+2D^{(i_{1}+1,i_{2})}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}&\text{if }i_{3}=1.\end{array}\right.

Also, we previously computed, see (
7.3
), that

\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2}-v_{3})_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}=-2M^{\mathbf{k}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}+2M^{\mathbf{k}(1,-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})},

where
M^{\mathbf{k}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}
is the endomorphism that is the identity on
\xi^{(k_{1})}(r_{1})\cap\xi^{(k_{2}+1)}(r_{2})
, and whose kernel is
\xi^{(k_{1}-1)}(r_{1})+\xi^{(k_{2})}(r_{2})
.

Since Proposition
7.18
(3) states that
\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{2}-v_{3})
is nilpotent, (
7.1
) implies

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)

\displaystyle=\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(u_{1})_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(v_{2}-v_{3})_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\big)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\frac{4i_{2}}{2n}+\frac{4(-i_{1}-1)}{2n}&\text{if }k_{1}=i_{1}+1\text{ and }i_{3}=1;\\
\frac{4(i_{2}+1)}{2n}-\frac{4(-i_{1})}{2n}&\text{if }k_{1}=i_{1}\text{ and }i_{3}=1;\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}=\mathbf{i}(1,0,-1);\\
2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}=\mathbf{i}(0,1,-1);\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.

We have thus shown that of all the fundamental blocks
D
such that
\mathbb{T}(D)\subset\widehat{\mathbb{T}}
, the only one where the sum

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)

possibly does not vanish is the fundamental block
D
such that
\pi(D)
is a boundary cylinder of
P
that has
\widehat{c}
as a boundary component. Even then,

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}=\mathbf{i}(1,0,-1);\\
2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}=\mathbf{i}(0,1,-1);\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Since we have also established that the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}})
is always zero, we have thus proven, as required, that

\omega_{[\rho]}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathbf{k}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{c}}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}=\mathbf{i}(1,0,-1);\\
2&\text{if }\mathbf{k}_{3}=\mathbf{i}(0,1,-1);\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

∎

Proposition 7.23
.

If
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}
are both length vector fields, then

\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{X}([\rho])\right)=0.

Proof.

By Proposition
7.21
, we know that if
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
is an eruption vector field and
\mathcal{W}
is either an eruption or lozenge vector field, then
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{W}^{\prime}([\rho]),\mathcal{W}([\rho])\right)=0
. Since the length vector fields are a sum of a lozenge vector field with some eruption vector fields, it suffices to show that

\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}([\rho])\right)=0

for some
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l}\in\mathcal{A}
and some
\widehat{\mathbf{c}},\widehat{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}^{o}
. Let
\widehat{c}
and
\widehat{c}^{\prime}
denote the non-isolated edge in
\mathcal{P}
underlying
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}
respectively.

Observe that lozenge vector fields are symmetric in every pair of pants of
\mathcal{P}
, so Lemma
7.13
(1) implies that the internal part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}([\rho])\right)
is zero. Thus, it is sufficient to prove that the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}([\rho])\right)
is also zero. We prove this in two cases.

Case 1:
\widehat{c}\neq\widehat{c}^{\prime}
.
For every fundamental block
D
, let
u_{1},\dots,u_{5},v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
be the edges in
D
as defined in Notation
7.15
. Then either
\widehat{c}^{\prime}
is not a boundary component of
\pi(D)
,
\widehat{c}
is not a boundary component of
\pi(D)
. Proposition
7.18
(4) then implies that
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{3}-v_{2})
and
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(u_{1})
are nilpotent in the former case, while
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{3}-v_{2})
and
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(u_{1})
are nilpotent in the latter case. Lemma
7.16
(2) and Lemma
7.17
(2) then implies that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.

Since
D
is arbitrary, this implies that the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}([\rho])\right)
is zero.

Case 2:
\widehat{c}=\widehat{c}^{\prime}
.
By the symmetry of lozenge vector fields, we may assume that
\widehat{\bf c}=\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}
. Observe that the same arguments used in Case 1 proves that if
D
is a fundamental block such that
\pi(D)
does not have
\widehat{c}
as a boundary component, then

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D)}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.

Let
D(1)
and
D(2)
be the fundamental blocks such that
\mathbb{T}(D(m))\subset\mathbb{T}_{\rm lift}
, and
\pi(D(1))
and
\pi(D(2))
are the boundary cylinders that share
\widehat{\bf c}
as a boundary component, and lie to the right and left of
{\widehat{\bf c}}
respectively. To show that the external part of
\omega_{[\rho]}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}}([\rho]),\mathcal{Z}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}([\rho])\right)
is zero, we now need only to prove that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D(1))}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)+\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D(2))}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.

For both
m=1,2
, let
P(m)
be the pair of pants of
\mathbb{P}
that contains
\pi(D(m))
, let
{\bf P}(m):=(P(m),\widehat{c})
, and let
(\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(m),\widehat{\mathbf{b}}^{\prime}(m))
be the pair of non-edge barriers associated to
{\bf P}(m)
. Then let
{\bf b}(m)
and
{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
be lifts to
\widetilde{S}
of
\widehat{\bf b}(m)
and
\widehat{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
respectively so that
{\bf b}(m)
intersects
\delta_{P(m)}
and
{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
intersects
\delta^{\prime}_{P(m)}
, and let
{\bf x}(m)
and
{\bf x}^{\prime}(m)
be the triples associated to
{\bf b}(m)
and
{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
respectively. By cyclically permuting the enumeration of the three vertices of
\mathbb{T}
in the interior of
P
if necessary, we may ensure that
e_{\delta_{P(m)},1}
intersects
{\bf b}(m)
and
e_{\delta^{\prime}_{P(m)},1}
intersects
{\bf b}^{\prime}(m)
. Let
u_{1}(m),\dots,u_{5}(m),v_{1}(m),\dots,v_{4}(m)
be the edges in
D(m)
as defined in Notation
7.15
, let
{\bf c}
be the lift of
\widehat{\bf c}
such that
D(1)\cap{\bf c}
and
D(2)\cap{\bf c}
are non-empty and coincide, and let
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},r_{2})
be the pair associated to
{\bf c}
.

Since lozenge vector fields are symmetric,

\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{2}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}=-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{3}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{2}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}=-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{3}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}

for both
m=1,2
, so

\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{3}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{3}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\big)-\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{2}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{2}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\big)=0.

Also, since
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{1}(m))=-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{4}(m))
, we see that

2\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{1}(m))=\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{1}(m)-v_{4}(m))=\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{2}(m)-v_{3}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}=2\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{2}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}.

Similarly,
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{1}(m))=-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{4}(m))
,

2\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{1}(m))=\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{2}(m)-v_{3}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}=2\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{2}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}.

Thus, (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) imply

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D(m))}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)

\displaystyle=2\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(u_{1}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{1}(m))\big)

\displaystyle-2\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{1}(m))\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(u_{1}(m))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\big).

Next, observe that
v_{1}(2)=v_{4}(1)
, so
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{1}(2))=-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{1}(1))
and
\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{1}(2))=-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{1}(1))
, so

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D(1))}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)+\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D(2))}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(e_{\delta,2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\tr\left(\left(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(u_{1}(1))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(u_{1}(2))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(v_{1}(1))\right)

\displaystyle+2\tr\left(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(v_{1}(1))\cdot\left(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(u_{1}(2))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(u_{1}(1))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}\right)\right).

By definition and Lemma
5.14
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(u_{1}(1))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}

\displaystyle=-\left(A^{{\mathbf{k}}_{3}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}\right)_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}+\left(A^{{\mathbf{k}}_{3}(1,-1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(1))}\right)_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}

\displaystyle=-2D^{\bf k}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}+2D^{(k_{1}+1,k_{2}-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(u_{1}(2))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}

\displaystyle=-\left(A^{\bar{\mathbf{k}}_{3}}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(2))}\right)_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}+\left(A^{\bar{\mathbf{k}}_{3}(1,-1,0)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{x}(2))}\right)_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}

\displaystyle=-2D^{\bf k}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})}+2D^{(k_{1}+1,k_{2}-1)}_{\xi_{\rho}(\mathbf{r})},

so

\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(u_{1}(1))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf k}_{\widehat{\bf c}^{\prime}}(u_{1}(2))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}=0.

Similarly,

\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(u_{1}(2))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}-\widetilde{\zeta}^{\bf l}_{\widehat{\bf c}}(u_{1}(1))_{{\bf r},{\rm diag}}=0.

It follows that

\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D(1))}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{1}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}^{2}(e_{\delta,2})\big)+\sum_{\delta\in\mathbb{T}(D(2))}\sgn(\delta)\tr\big(\widetilde{\nu}^{1}(e_{\delta,1})\cdot\widetilde{\nu}^{2}(e_{\delta,2})\big)=0.\qed
